Support point 129: repair measured equal-weight marginal deficits
Support point identifier: 129

Survey context: Imagine a nationally representative 2026 survey of US teen girls. Predict the response distribution for the population named in the survey item. These candidate questions were not observed in the American Teenagers Survey and are being used to study whether frontier-model consensus can supply explicitly synthetic calibration targets.
Battery topic: social-media habits and experiences among US teen girls

Construct an additional synthetic survey-response profile for a support bank. Its purpose is to occupy a response pattern
that is underrepresented in the measured bank. Treat every item-specific tendency below as deliberate; do not replace the
pattern with one uniformly positive or negative outlook.

Target response tendencies:
- social_media_hours: lean toward "Less than one hour" for Daily time on social media
- posting_frequency: lean toward "Less than once a week" for Posting or sharing frequency
- late_night_use: lean toward "Never" for Social-media use after intended bedtime
- sleep_interference: lean toward "Often" for Social-media interference with sleep
- social_comparison: lean toward "Sometimes" for Negative social comparison
- connection_effect: lean toward "Makes little difference" for Effect on connection to friends
- parental_rules: lean toward "Some clear rules" for Strictness of parental social-media rules
- primary_platform: lean toward "Instagram" for Primary social-media platform

Write a concise persona that could make this combination of responses coherent without inventing demographic
characteristics. Address the persona in the second person, beginning with "Your views..." or "You...". Include only the
persona description, not instructions about answering or probabilities. Then provide subjective response probabilities
for every item. Put more probability on each targeted option than on the alternatives, while preserving genuine
uncertainty. Each vector must follow the displayed option order, contain nonnegative numbers, sum to 1, and assign at least
0.01 to every option.

Items and response options:
- social_media_hours: Daily time on social media
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than one hour; 2. One to three hours; 3. Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Posting or sharing frequency
  Options, in output order: 1. Never or almost never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. A few times a week; 4. At least once a day
- late_night_use: Social-media use after intended bedtime
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- sleep_interference: Social-media interference with sleep
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- social_comparison: Negative social comparison
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- connection_effect: Effect on connection to friends
  Options, in output order: 1. Makes me feel less connected; 2. Makes little difference; 3. Makes me feel more connected
- parental_rules: Strictness of parental social-media rules
  Options, in output order: 1. No rules; 2. A few loose rules; 3. Some clear rules; 4. Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Primary social-media platform
  Options, in output order: 1. TikTok; 2. Instagram; 3. YouTube; 4. Snapchat; 5. Another platform; 6. I do not use social media

Return only valid JSON with exactly this schema:
{
  "persona": "Your views on ...",
  "probabilities": {
    "social_media_hours": [numbers in option order],
    "posting_frequency": [numbers in option order],
    "late_night_use": [numbers in option order],
    "sleep_interference": [numbers in option order],
    "social_comparison": [numbers in option order],
    "connection_effect": [numbers in option order],
    "parental_rules": [numbers in option order],
    "primary_platform": [numbers in option order]
  }
}

Survey context: Imagine a nationally representative 2026 survey of US teen girls. Predict the response distribution for the population named in the survey item. These candidate questions were not observed in the American Teenagers Survey and are being used to study whether frontier-model consensus can supply explicitly synthetic calibration targets.
Battery topic: social-media habits and experiences among US teen girls

Construct an additional synthetic survey-response profile for a support bank. Its purpose is to occupy a response pattern
that is underrepresented in the measured bank. Treat every item-specific tendency below as deliberate; do not replace the
pattern with one uniformly positive or negative outlook.

Target response tendencies:
- social_media_hours: lean toward "One to three hours" for Daily time on social media
- posting_frequency: lean toward "A few times a week" for Posting or sharing frequency
- late_night_use: lean toward "Often" for Social-media use after intended bedtime
- sleep_interference: lean toward "Often" for Social-media interference with sleep
- social_comparison: lean toward "Never" for Negative social comparison
- connection_effect: lean toward "Makes me feel more connected" for Effect on connection to friends
- parental_rules: lean toward "No rules" for Strictness of parental social-media rules
- primary_platform: lean toward "Snapchat" for Primary social-media platform

Write a concise persona that could make this combination of responses coherent without inventing demographic
characteristics. Address the persona in the second person, beginning with "Your views..." or "You...". Include only the
persona description, not instructions about answering or probabilities. Then provide subjective response probabilities
for every item. Put more probability on each targeted option than on the alternatives, while preserving genuine
uncertainty. Each vector must follow the displayed option order, contain nonnegative numbers, sum to 1, and assign at least
0.01 to every option.

Items and response options:
- social_media_hours: Daily time on social media
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than one hour; 2. One to three hours; 3. Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Posting or sharing frequency
  Options, in output order: 1. Never or almost never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. A few times a week; 4. At least once a day
- late_night_use: Social-media use after intended bedtime
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- sleep_interference: Social-media interference with sleep
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- social_comparison: Negative social comparison
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- connection_effect: Effect on connection to friends
  Options, in output order: 1. Makes me feel less connected; 2. Makes little difference; 3. Makes me feel more connected
- parental_rules: Strictness of parental social-media rules
  Options, in output order: 1. No rules; 2. A few loose rules; 3. Some clear rules; 4. Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Primary social-media platform
  Options, in output order: 1. TikTok; 2. Instagram; 3. YouTube; 4. Snapchat; 5. Another platform; 6. I do not use social media

Return only valid JSON with exactly this schema:
{
  "persona": "Your views on ...",
  "probabilities": {
    "social_media_hours": [numbers in option order],
    "posting_frequency": [numbers in option order],

- social_media_hours: lean toward "Four or more hours" for Daily time on social media
- posting_frequency: lean toward "Never or almost never" for Posting or sharing frequency
- late_night_use: lean toward "Never" for Social-media use after intended bedtime
- sleep_interference: lean toward "Often" for Social-media interference with sleep
- social_comparison: lean toward "Never" for Negative social comparison
- connection_effect: lean toward "Makes little difference" for Effect on connection to friends
- parental_rules: lean toward "No rules" for Strictness of parental social-media rules

  Options, in output order: 1. Less than one hour; 2. One to three hours; 3. Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Posting or sharing frequency
  Options, in output order: 1. Never or almost never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. A few times a week; 4. At least once a day
- late_night_use: Social-media use after intended bedtime

- sleep_interference: Social-media interference with sleep
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- social_comparison: Negative social comparison

- connection_effect: Effect on connection to friends
  Options, in output order: 1. Makes me feel less connected; 2. Makes little difference; 3. Makes me feel more connected
- parental_rules: Strictness of parental social-media rules
  Options, in output order: 1. No rules; 2. A few loose rules; 3. Some clear rules; 4. Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Primary social-media platform
  Options, in output order: 1. TikTok; 2. Instagram; 3. YouTube; 4. Snapchat; 5. Another platform; 6. I do not use social media

These candidate questions were not observed in the American Teenagers Survey and are being used to study whether frontier-model consensus can supply explicitly synthetic calibration targets.
Battery topic: social-media habits and experiences among US teen girls

Construct an additional synthetic survey-response profile for a support bank. Its purpose is to occupy a response pattern
that is underrepresented in the measured bank. Treat every item-specific tendency below as deliberate; do not replace the
pattern with one uniformly positive or negative outlook.

Target response tendencies:
- social_media_hours: lean toward "One to three hours" for Daily time on social media
- posting_frequency: lean toward "Never or almost never" for Posting or sharing frequency
- late_night_use: lean toward "Rarely" for Social-media use after intended bedtime
- sleep_interference: lean toward "Often" for Social-media interference with sleep
- social_comparison: lean toward "Never" for Negative social comparison
- connection_effect: lean toward "Makes little difference" for Effect on connection to friends
- parental_rules: lean toward "A few loose rules" for Strictness of parental social-media rules
- primary_platform: lean toward "I do not use social media" for Primary social-media platform

Write a concise persona that could make this combination of responses coherent without inventing demographic
characteristics. Address the persona in the second person, beginning with "Your views..." or "You...". Include only the
persona description, not instructions about answering or probabilities. Then provide subjective response probabilities
for every item. Put more probability on each targeted option than on the alternatives, while preserving genuine
uncertainty. Each vector must follow the displayed option order, contain nonnegative numbers, sum to 1, and assign at least
0.01 to every option.

Items and response options:
- social_media_hours: Daily time on social media
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than one hour; 2. One to three hours; 3. Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Posting or sharing frequency
  Options, in output order: 1. Never or almost never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. A few times a week; 4. At least once a day
- late_night_use: Social-media use after intended bedtime
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- sleep_interference: Social-media interference with sleep
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- social_comparison: Negative social comparison
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- connection_effect: Effect on connection to friends
  Options, in output order: 1. Makes me feel less connected; 2. Makes little difference; 3. Makes me feel more connected
- parental_rules: Strictness of parental social-media rules
  Options, in output order: 1. No rules; 2. A few loose rules; 3. Some clear rules; 4. Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Primary social-media platform
  Options, in output order: 1. TikTok; 2. Instagram; 3. YouTube; 4. Snapchat; 5. Another platform; 6. I do not use social media

Return only valid JSON with exactly this schema:
{
  "persona": "Your views on ...",
  "probabilities": {
    "social_media_hours": [numbers in option order],
    "posting_frequency": [numbers in option order],
    "late_night_use": [numbers in option order],
    "sleep_interference": [numbers in option order],
    "social_comparison": [numbers in option order],
    "connection_effect": [numbers in option order],
    "parental_rules": [numbers in option order],
    "primary_platform": [numbers in option order]
  }
}
